+# Q4 AI usage log
+
+The primary implementation was performed by Codex, model **gpt-6.1-sol**, through the Codex application. The locally available Codex CLI reports **0.164.0** (`../q5/evidence/codex-version.txt`); this is the CLI version, not a claim about the application's build. There was no human-written implementation in this experiment.
+
+The user's verbatim request and all visible primary-session responses, tool calls, and outputs are preserved in the shared appendix [`../q5/evidence/primary-session.jsonl`](../q5/evidence/primary-session.jsonl). Its export is a snapshot through final verification, rather than an export of private reasoning or system instructions. The orchestrator's task is `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.orch/school-dev-hw2-q45.md`; a verbatim snapshot is [`../q5/evidence/assignment-prompt.txt`](../q5/evidence/assignment-prompt.txt). The task directed me to characterize real code before refactoring, retain actual evidence, and perform review and security checks.
+
+Main recorded steps:
+
+1. Read the assignment, requirements, and shared decisions; verified an isolated worktree and branch.
+2. Chose Requests and pinned a historical commit shared with Q5. Inspected digest authentication and relevant upstream tests.
+3. Captured 29 golden cases from unchanged source and ran characterization plus upstream digest tests successfully.
+4. Replaced duplicate digest closures with one constructor table and closure; reran the same tests successfully and saved the actual source diff.
+5. Repeated the experiment from a fresh clone using the submitted patch and reproduction script.
+6. Invoked `codex review --base main`; evaluated its findings, simplified the submission, and audited logs and execution locations. Results and complete review output are linked from [review.md](review.md).
+
+No independent implementer was used for Q4. The separate Q5 session is documented in its own usage log. All implementation and reported experimental observations remain AI-assisted; the golden cases were recorded from the original source, not invented outputs.

+# Q4 — Refactoring Requests digest authentication
+
+Requests is an established, actively maintained Python HTTP library, with a substantial implementation and upstream test suite: [repository](https://github.com/psf/requests). Both experiments pin commit **`7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a`** (2024-02-20). The historical snapshot makes Q5 reproducible; it is not the current release. Upstream source and environments live outside this submission under `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/`.
+
+## Why this refactoring
+
+`src/requests/auth.py:HTTPDigestAuth.build_digest_header` mixes algorithm selection, text encoding, digest calculation, challenge negotiation, and nonce state. Its four algorithm branches each define almost identical UTF-8 conversion and hexadecimal-digest functions. A future change to input conversion would require four edits. This is a concrete duplication problem in a real authentication path.
+
+`refactor.patch`, generated with `git diff -- src/requests/auth.py`, replaces the branch-specific functions with a five-entry constructor table and one local `hash_utf8` function. MD5-SESS deliberately shares the MD5 constructor; its session-specific calculation remains in the existing code. The function shrinks by 27 lines. No new algorithms or protocol behavior are introduced.
+
+## Tests before implementation
+
+I recorded 29 golden cases from the **unmodified** pinned source, then wrote `tests/test_digest_characterization.py`. Each case becomes a named `unittest` test. The committed `tests/digest_golden.json` contains literal observations, not values recalculated by the refactored code. Time and random bytes are fixed; tests compare the entire returned Authorization header and the nonce counter/last-nonce state.
+
+Coverage includes:
+
+- Default, MD5, MD5-SESS, SHA, SHA-256, SHA-512, including lowercase algorithm names.
+- Missing/empty qop, `auth`, and multiple offered qop values; unsupported qop and whitespace behavior.
+- Unicode credentials/realm, opaque values, an empty URL path, queries, fragments, and POST.
+- Repeated and changed nonces, unknown/empty algorithms, and MD5-SESS without qop.
+
+These are characterization tests: even the existing whitespace limitation is preserved. They do not prove every possible input or authenticate against every server.
+
+## Actual results
+
+| Phase | Own characterization tests | Upstream `tests/test_lowlevel.py -k digest` |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| Before source change | 29 passed | 3 passed, 17 deselected |
+| After refactoring | 29 passed | 3 passed, 17 deselected |
+
+The upstream tests exercise real loopback HTTP digest challenges, retry limits, redirects, and response-status handling. Logs with commands, commit and source paths are in `evidence/before-*.txt` and `evidence/after-*.txt`. `evidence/final-verification.txt` independently repeats both phases in a new clone and checks that the submitted patch applies. This is a targeted upstream run, not a claim that the entire Requests suite passed.
+
+## Reproduce
+
+Use Python 3.14.4. Requests' historical development requirements pin an old pytest; instead this experiment uses pytest 9.1.1 on Python 3.14. The exact installed packages are in `evidence/requirements-lock.txt`. The editable installation was built with setuptools 80.10.2 and wheel 0.48.0; install those before `pip install --no-build-isolation -e PATH_TO_REQUESTS`.
+
+With the existing cached source and environment:
+
+```bash
+python3 hw2/q4/reproduce.py \
+  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/requests-original \
+  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/venv/bin/python
+```
+
+For another machine, clone `https://github.com/psf/requests.git` into a cache outside the submission, install `charset-normalizer`, `idna`, `urllib3`, `certifi`, `PySocks`, and `pytest` into a Python 3.14 environment, and substitute those two paths. The script pins the commit, creates a new sibling clone, runs tests before/after applying `refactor.patch`, and leaves the input checkout untouched. It sets `PYTHONPATH` to each tested clone to avoid accidentally testing another editable installation.
+
+AI involvement and the review are disclosed in [ai-usage-log.md](ai-usage-log.md). Review findings and security checks are recorded in [review.md](review.md).

@@ -0,0 +1,39 @@
+"""Run from anywhere: python3 reproduce.py CACHED_REQUESTS PYTHON_EXECUTABLE.
+
+Creates a disposable clone beside the cached source; never modifies the input.
+The supplied Python needs Requests' dependencies and pytest installed.
+"""
+import os
+from pathlib import Path
+import subprocess
+import sys
+import tempfile
+
+BASE = '7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a'
+HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+
+
+def main():
+    upstream = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve()
+    python = str(Path(sys.argv[2]).absolute())
+    scratch = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='q4-verify-', dir=upstream.parent)) / 'requests'
+    subprocess.run(['git', 'clone', '--quiet', '--no-hardlinks', str(upstream), str(scratch)], check=True)
+    subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(scratch), 'checkout', '--quiet', '--detach', BASE], check=True)
+    env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONPATH=str(scratch / 'src'), PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD='1')
+    for phase in ('before', 'after'):
+        print(f'\n{phase}: {scratch} at {BASE}', flush=True)
+        for command in (
+            [python, '-m', 'unittest', 'discover', '-s', str(HERE / 'tests'), '-v'],
+            [python, '-m', 'pytest', 'tests/test_lowlevel.py', '-k', 'digest', '-q', '-o', 'addopts='],
+        ):
+            print(f'Command: {command!r}', flush=True)
+            subprocess.run(command, cwd=scratch, env=env, check=True)
+        if phase == 'before':
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', '--check', str(HERE / 'refactor.patch')], cwd=scratch, check=True)
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', str(HERE / 'refactor.patch')], cwd=scratch, check=True)
+    subprocess.run(['git', 'diff', '--check'], cwd=scratch, check=True)
+    print('Q4: original and refactored behavior agree; upstream digest tests pass.', flush=True)
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    main()

+"""Golden behavior captured before changing Requests' digest implementation.
+
+No network access: pin time and randomness, then compare entire public headers
+and the nonce state after each call. See digest_golden.json for all 29 cases.
+"""
+import json
+from pathlib import Path
+import unittest
+from unittest.mock import patch
+
+from requests.auth import HTTPDigestAuth
+
+
+class DigestCharacterization(unittest.TestCase):
+    pass
+
+
+def make_test(case):
+    def test(self):
+        auth = HTTPDigestAuth(case.get('username', 'user'), case.get('password', 'pass'))
+        auth.init_per_thread_state()
+        challenge = {'realm': case.get('realm', 'realm'), 'nonce': 'nonce-A'}
+        for key in ('algorithm', 'qop', 'opaque'):
+            if case.get(key) is not None:
+                challenge[key] = case[key]
+        auth._thread_local.chal = challenge
+        with patch('requests.auth.os.urandom', return_value=b'12345678'), patch(
+            'requests.auth.time.ctime', return_value='Mon Jan 01 00:00:00 2024'
+        ):
+            for nonce, expected in zip(case.get('nonces', ['nonce-A']), case['observations'], strict=True):
+                challenge['nonce'] = nonce
+                with self.subTest(nonce=nonce):
+                    actual = auth.build_digest_header(
+                        case.get('method', 'GET'),
+                        case.get('url', 'https://example.test/resource?x=1'),
+                    )
+                    self.assertEqual(actual, expected['header'])
+                    self.assertEqual(auth._thread_local.nonce_count, expected['nonce_count'])
+                    self.assertEqual(auth._thread_local.last_nonce, expected['last_nonce'])
+    return test
+
+
+for index, case in enumerate(json.loads(Path(__file__).with_name('digest_golden.json').read_text())):
+    setattr(DigestCharacterization, f'test_{index:02d}_{case["name"].replace("-", "_")}', make_test(case))
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    unittest.main()

+Repair the following bug in this Requests checkout.
+
+Symptom: when a Response contains invalid JSON, the JSON decoding exception raised by Response.json() cannot survive a pickle.dumps / pickle.loads round trip: deserialization raises TypeError about missing constructor arguments. This also causes exceptions returned by worker processes to break a ProcessPoolExecutor. The exception should remain catchable as Requests' JSON decoding exception and preserve the JSON error message, document, and position after serialization.
+
+Work independently using only this checkout. Do not read Git history, other checkouts, assignment artifacts, or online fixes. Do not access the network, install packages, commit, or send anything externally. Make the repair and verify it locally. Use /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/venv/bin/python (Python 3.14); set PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src so imports resolve to this checkout rather than the editable install in another directory. Pytest and its mock plugin are already available. In your final answer explain the change and what you ran.

+# 小弟任務單：HW2 Q4（既有系統重構）與 Q5（既有系統修 bug＋與 AI agent 比較）
+
+你是 software-testing 的開發小弟（agent 名 `school-dev-hw2-q45`，看板卡片 `K2`），由 orch（另一個 Claude session）派來。你只負責這一件事，在自己的 worktree 裡做完。回覆用繁體中文；程式碼、commit、繳交文件用英文。
+
+## 任務
+先讀 `hw2/requirement.txt`（題目全文）與 `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.orch/hw2-decisions.md`。只做 **Question 4 和 Question 5**，目標是拿滿分。
+
+**選系統**：挑一個真正的大型、活躍的開源 Python 專案（純 Python、`pip install -e .` 就能跑測試的，例如 requests、attrs、click、more-itertools 等級；自己判斷）。clone 到 `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/<專案>`（repo 外，不要把整個專案 commit 進來）。固定一個 commit hash 並寫進文件。不要對上游開 PR、issue 或留言。
+
+**Q4（重構）**：在那個系統裡找一段真的值得重構的程式（說明為什麼）。先寫 characterization tests 捕捉相關行為、在原始碼上全綠；重構；再跑一次全綠，也跑上游自己的相關測試。繳交物放 `hw2/q4/`：`tests/`（你寫的測試）、`refactor.patch`（`git diff` 產生）、`evidence/`（重構前後的真實測試輸出）、`report.md`（系統與 commit、選這段的理由、測試捕捉了哪些行為、重構內容、結果）。
+
+**Q5（修 fault）**：找一個真實存在過的 bug（建議從上游 issue tracker／changelog 找一個已修好的，checkout 到修好之前的 commit；或自己找到一個現存 bug）。
+1. 你自己：找出要改的程式，先寫測試捕捉現行行為，至少一個測試要失敗、證明你找到 fault；修好；全部通過。
+2. 再從原始有 bug 的程式碼（乾淨的 checkout），開一個**全新的** AI coding agent session 請它修同一個 bug：用 `codex exec`（全新 session，不帶你的 context），提示只描述症狀，不告訴它你怎麼修。完整保存：工具名稱、模型、版本（`codex --version`）、每一個 prompt 原文、agent 的完整回應（含它跑的指令與輸出）。
+3. 報告它有沒有先寫重現 fault 的測試再改程式（照它的實際紀錄判斷），比較它的修法和你的。
+繳交物放 `hw2/q5/`：`tests/`、`my-fix.patch`、`agent-fix.patch`、`evidence/`、`report.md`、`ai-usage-log.md`（題目要求的 AI usage log：Q5 用了哪些 AI、每個 prompt 原文、完整回應）。
+
+**Q4 也要 AI usage log**：你本身就是 AI agent，Q4 是你做的；在 `hw2/q4/ai-usage-log.md` 照實寫工具、模型、版本，以及 orch 給你的任務（本檔路徑即可）與主要步驟摘要。不要捏造任何沒發生的事。
+
+不做：其他題、截圖、GitHub push。
+
+## 開工
+1. 確認你在自己的 worktree、自己的 branch：
+   `[ "$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir)" != "$(git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)" ] && git symbolic-ref --short HEAD`
+   要印出你的 branch。沒印出來就什麼都不要改，回報 `blocked` 等 orch。
+
+## 流程（每一步做完都回報）
+1. 開發：Q4、Q5。
+2. Code review 一輪：`codex review --base main`。逐條判斷，成立就修，不成立記理由。
+3. 自我精簡：繳交文件要清楚、不灌水。
+4. Security 自審：clone 的上游程式碼只在 `.cache` 裡跑測試；不要執行來路不明的安裝腳本；log 裡不能有 token。
+5. 全部你寫的測試跑一次（原始碼上應失敗的照預期失敗、修好後全綠），回報 done。
+
+沒有 remote，**不要 push、不要開 PR**。完成時 `--result` 填你的 branch 名與 worktree 絕對路徑。
+
+## 回報（orch 只靠這個知道你做到哪）
+```
+/home/yuankai/.claude/skills/orch/scripts/report.sh <stage|blocked|question|done> "<白話一句>" --card K2 [--stage dev] [--result <路徑>] [--needs 決定|看|無]
+```
+- **做完一定要寫 `done`**（附 `--result` 和 `--needs`）。
+- 白話一句要讓人一眼看懂。
+- 在你自己的 worktree 維護 `.orch/status.md`（`stage:`、`summary:`、`pr:`，加 `## log`）。`.orch/` 不 commit。
+
+## 提問
+要決定的事：寫進 worktree 的 `.orch/questions.md`（問題／選 A／選 B／我建議），跑 `report.sh question "<一句話>" --card K2`，結束這一輪等 orch。小事自己判斷，記在 log。
+
+## 先問，不要自己做
+- merge、push、打 tag、改 main
+- 在 GitHub（含上游專案）或任何外部服務留言、開 issue／PR、發訊息；刪除 `.cache` 以外的任何東西
+- 不 commit、不印出密鑰與 token
+- 額度用完：回報 `blocked "額度用完"`，orch 會幫你換帳號
+
+## 同時在跑的其他工作（注意衝突）
+K1 `school-dev-hw2-q1`：動 `hw2/calc/`、`hw2/q1/`。你不要碰這兩個資料夾。

requests、attrs、click、more-itertools 等級；自己判斷）。clone 到 `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/<專案>`（repo 外，不要把整個專案 commit 進來）。固定一個 commit hash 並寫進文件。不要對上游開 PR、issue 或留言。\\n\\n**Q4（重構）**：在那個系統裡找一段真的值得重構的程式（說明為什麼）。先寫 characterization tests 捕捉相關行為、在原始碼上全綠；重構；再跑一次全綠，也跑上游自己的相關測試。繳交物放 `hw2/q4/`：`tests/`（你寫的測試）、`refactor.patch`（`git diff` 產生）、`evidence/`（重構前後的真實測試輸出）、`report.md`（系統與 commit、選這段的理由、測試捕捉了哪些行為、重構內容、結果）。\\n\\n**Q5（修 fault）**：找一個真實存在過的 bug（建議從上游 issue tracker／changelog 找一個已修好的，checkout 到修好之前的 commit；或自己找到一個現存 bug）。\\n1. 你自己：找出要改的程式，先寫測試捕捉現行行為，至少一個測試要失敗、證明你找到 fault；修好；全部通過。\\n2. 再從原始有 bug 的程式碼（乾淨的 checkout），開一個**全新的** AI coding agent session 請它修同一個 bug：用 `codex exec`（全新 session，不帶你的 `hw2/q5/`：`tests/`、`my-fix.patch`、`agent-fix.patch`、`evidence/`、`report.md`、`ai-usage-log.md`（題目要求的 AI usage log：Q5 用了哪些 AI、每個 prompt 原文、完整回應）。\\n\\n**Q4 也要 AI usage log**：你本身就是 AI agent，Q4 是你做的；在 Attempt 3 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n\\n# [BUG] JSONDecodeError can't be deserialized - invalid JSON raises a BrokenProcessPool and crashes the entire process pool #6628\\n\\nNew issue\\n\\nCopy link\\n\\nNew issue\\n\\nCopy link\\n\\nClosed\\n\\nClosed\\n\\n[BUG] JSONDecodeError can't be deserialized - invalid JSON raises a BrokenProcessPool and crashes the entire process pool#6628\\n\\nCopy link\\n\\nMilestone\\n\\n2.32.0\\n\\n## Description\\n\\nTarty\\n\\nopened on Jan 31, 2024\\n\\nIssue body actions\\n\\nHi all,\\n\\nI've stumbled upon a bug in the `requests` library, and have a proposal for a fix.\\n\\nIn short: I have a process pool running tasks in parallel, that are among other things doing queries to third-party APIs. One third-party returns an invalid JSON document as response in case of error.\\n\\nHowever, instead of just having a JSONDecodeError as the result of my job, the entire process pool crashes due to a BrokenProcessPool error with the following stack trace:\\n    \\n    `Traceback (most recent call last):\\n      File \\\"/usr/local/lib/python3.11/concurrent/futures/process.py\\\", line 424, in wait_result_broken_or_wakeup\\n        result_item = result_reader.recv()\\n                      ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\\n      File \\\"/usr/local/lib/python3.11/multiprocessing/connection.py\\\", line 251, in recv\\n        return _ForkingPickler.loads(buf.getbuffer())\\n               ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^\\n      File \\\"/usr/local/lib/python3.11/site-packages/requests/exceptions.py\\\", line 41, in __init__\\n        CompatJSONDecodeError.__init__(self, *args)\\n    TypeError: JSONDecodeError.__init__() missing 2 required positional arguments: 'doc' and 'pos'\\n    `\\n\\nSo I'm in a situation where receiving one invalid JSON as response is interrupting all my ongoing tasks because the entire process pool is crashing, and no more tasks can be submitted until the process pool recovered.\\n\\n## Origin of the bug + Fix\\n\\nAfter investigation, this is because the `requests.exception.JSONDecodeError` instances can't be deserialized once they've been serialized via `pickle`. So when the main process is trying to deserialize the error returned by the child process, the main process is crashing with to the error above.\\n\\nI think this bug has been around for a while, I've found old tickets from different projects mentioning issues that are looking similar: celery/celery#5712\\n\\nI've pinpointed the bug to the following class: https://github.com/psf/requests/blob/main/src/requests/exceptions.py#L31\\n\\nBasically, due the MRO/order of inheritance, the `__reduce__` method used will not be the one of `CompatJSONDecodeError`. Most of the args will therefore be ditched when pickling the instance and it can't be deserialised back because `CompatJSONDecodeError.__init__` does expect those args. MRO below:\\n    \\n    `In [1]: from requests.exceptions import JSONDecodeError\\n    \\n    In [2]: JSONDecodeError.__mro__\\n    Out[2]:\\n    (requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError,\\n     requests.exceptions.InvalidJSONError,\\n     requests.exceptions.RequestException,\\n     OSError,\\n     simplejson.errors.JSONDecodeError,\\n     ValueError,\\n     Exception,\\n     BaseException,\\n     object)\\n    `\\n\\nI think the fix could be quite simple and should have very little side-effects: to specify a `JSONDecodeError.__reduce__` method that will call the one from the correct parent class (it will be regardless that it is json/simplejson via the Compat class, their respective methods having different signatures).\\n\\nI've taken the initiative to write a fix + a test and will raise a pull request to that effect 🙏\\n\\n* * *\\n\\n## Expected Result\\n\\nI've written a test for this case: the error can easily be reproduced by simply trying to `pickle.dumps()` then `pickle.loads()` on a error:\\n    \\n    json_decode_error = requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError(\\n        \\\"Extra data\\\",\\n        '{\\\"responseCode\\\":[\\\"706\\\"],\\\"data\\\":null}{\\\"responseCode\\\":[\\\"706\\\"],\\\"data\\\":null}',\\n        36,\\n    )\\n    deserialized_error = pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(json_decode_error))\\n    assert repr(json_decode_error) == repr(deserialized_error)\\n\\nThis assertion should be true\\n\\n## Actual Result\\n\\nCurrently, instead of passing it'll raise the following error:\\n    \\n    `>       CompatJSONDecodeError.__init__(self, *args)\\n    E       TypeError: JSONDecodeError.__init__() missing 2 required positional arguments: 'doc' and 'pos'\\n    `\\n\\n## Reproduction Steps\\n\\nAs mentioned above, this bug is more impactful in a multi-process architecture as it'll break the entire process pool.\\nFor something looking a bit more like a live-case, I've produced a little snippet with a really simple API returning an invalid JSON:\\n    \\n    # File api.py\\n    \\n    from fastapi import FastAPI\\n    from starlette.responses import PlainTextResponse\\n    \\n    app = FastAPI()\\n    \\n    \\n    @app.get(\\\"/\\\")\\n    async def root():\\n        # An invalid json string returned by the endpoint that will trigger a JSONDecodeError when calling `res.json()`\\n        s = '{\\\"responseCode\\\":[\\\"706\\\"],\\\"data\\\":null}{\\\"responseCode\\\":[\\\"706\\\"],\\\"data\\\":null}'\\n        return PlainTextResponse(s, media_type=\\\"application/json\\\", status_code=400)\\n    \\n    \\n    # Run the API:\\n    # $ uvicorn api:app --reload\\n    #\\n    # curl http://127.0.0.1:8000 will return the invalid json\\n\\nand the following\\n    \\n    from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor\\n    from concurrent.futures.process import BrokenProcessPool\\n    \\n    import requests\\n    \\n    \\n    def my_task():\\n        response = requests.get('http://127.0.0.1:8000/')\\n        response.json()\\n    \\n    def my_main_func():\\n        with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=4) as executor:\\n            future = executor.submit(my_task)\\n            for i in range(0, 5):\\n                try:\\n                    future.result(timeout=100)\\n                    print(f\\\"Attempt {i} ok\\\")\\n                except BrokenProcessPool:\\n                    print(f\\\"Attempt {i} - the pool is broken\\\")\\n                except requests.JSONDecodeError:\\n                    print(f\\\"Attempt {i} raises a request JSONDecodeError\\\")\\n    \\n    \\n    if __name__ == '__main__':\\n        my_main_func()\\n\\nInstead of getting the following output:\\n    \\n    `Attempt 0 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n    Attempt 1 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n    Attempt 2 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n    Attempt 3 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n    Attempt 4 raises a request JSONDecodeError\\n    `\\n\\nOne would currently have:\\n    \\n    `Attempt 0 - the pool is broken\\n    Attempt 1 - the pool is broken\\n    Attempt 2 - the pool is broken\\n    Attempt 3 - the pool is broken\\n    Attempt 4 - the pool is broken\\n    `\\n\\nAn invalid JSON is crashing the entire process pool and no job can be submitted anymore.\\n\\n## System Information\\n\\nTested with:\\n\\n  * request==2.31.0\\n  * Python==3.11.7\\n\\n* * *\\n\\nThanks a lot for taking the time to read this long bug report!!--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nrequests/HISTORY.md at main · psf/requests · GitHub The source files for the projects (formerly `requests`) is now located in `src/requests` in the Requests sdist. (#6506)\\n  * Starting in Requests 2.33.0, Requests will migrate to a PEP 517 build system using `hatchling`. This should not impact the average user, but extremely old versions of packaging utilities may have issues with the new packaging format.\\n\\n## 2.31.0 (2023-05-22)\\n\\nSecurity\\n\\n  * Versions of Requests between v2.3.0 and v2.30.0 are vulnerable to potential forwarding of `Proxy-Authorization` headers to destination servers when following HTTPS redirects.\\n\\n## 2.28.1 (2022-06-29)\\n\\nImprovements\\n\\n  * Speed optimization in `iter_content` with transition to `yield from`. (#6170)\\n\\nDependencies\\n\\n  * Added support for chardet 5.0.0 (#6179)\\n  * Added support for charset-normalizer 2.1.0 (#6169)\\n\\n## 2.28.0 (2022-06-09)\\n\\nDeprecations\\n\\n  * ⚠️ Requests has officially dropped support for Python 2.7. ⚠️ (#6091)\\n  * Requests has officially dropped support for Python 3.6 (including pypy3.6). (#6091)\\n\\nImprovements\\n\\n  * Wrap JSON parsing issues in Request's JSONDecodeError for payloads without an encoding to make `json()` API consistent. (#6097)\\n  * Parse header components consistently, raising an InvalidHeader error in all invalid cases. (#6154)\\n  * Added provisional 3.11 support with current beta build. (#6155)\\n  * Requests got a makeover and we decided to paint it black. (#6095)\\n\\nBugfixes\\n\\n  * Fixed bug where setting `CURL_CA_BUNDLE` to an empty string would disable cert verification. All Requests 2.x versions before 2.28.0 are affected. (#6074)\\n  * Fixed urllib3 exception leak, wrapping `urllib3.exceptions.SSLError` with `requests.exceptions.SSLError` for `content` and `iter_content`. (#6057)\\n  * Fixed issue where invalid Windows registry entries caused proxy resolution to raise an exception rather than ignoring the entry. (#6149)\\n  * Fixed issue where entire payload could be included in the error message for JSONDecodeError. (#6036)\\n\\n## 2.27.1 (2022-01-05)\\n\\nBugfixes\\n\\n  * Fixed parsing issue that resulted in the `auth` component being dropped from proxy URLs. (#6028)\\n\\n## 2.27.0 (2022-01-03)\\n\\nImprovements\\n\\n  * Officially added support for Python 3.10. (#5928)\\n\\n  * Added a `requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError` to unify JSON exceptions between Python 2 and 3. This gets raised in the `response.json()` method, and is backwards compatible as it inherits from previously thrown exceptions. Can be caught from `requests.exceptions.RequestException` as well. (#5856)\\n\\n  * Improved error text for misnamed `InvalidSchema` and `MissingSchema` exceptions. This is a temporary fix until exceptions can be renamed (Schema->Scheme). (#6017)\\n\\n  * Improved proxy parsing for proxy URLs missing a scheme. This will address recent changes to `urlparse` in Python 3.9+. \\nBugfixes\\n\\n  * Fixed occasional `TypeError` when attempting to decode a JSON response that occurred in an error case. Now correctly returns a `ValueError`.\\n  * Requests would incorrectly ignore a non-CIDR IP address in the `NO_PROXY` environment variables: Requests now treats it as a specific IP.\\n  * Fixed a bug when sending JSON data that could cause us to encounter obscure OpenSSL errors in certain network conditions (yes, really).\\n  * Added type checks to ensure that `iter_content` only accepts integers and `None` for chunk sizes.\\n  * Fixed issue where responses whose body had not been fully consumed would have the underlying connection closed but not returned to the connection pool, which could cause Requests to hang in situations where the `HTTPAdapter` had been configured to use a blocking connection pool.\\n\\nMiscellaneous\\n\\n  * Updated bundled urllib3 to 1.16.\\n  * Some previous releases accidentally accepted non-strings as acceptable header values. This release does not.\\n\\n## 2.10.0 (2016-04-29)\\n\\nNew Features\\n\\n  * SOCKS Proxy Support! (requires PySocks; `$ pip install requests[socks]`)\\n\\nMiscellaneous\\n\\n  * Updated bundled urllib3 to 1.15.1.\\n\\n## 2.9.2 (2016-04-29)\\n\\nImprovements\\n\\n  * Change built-in CaseInsensitiveDict (used for headers) to use OrderedDict as its underlying datastore.\\n\\nBugfixes\\n\\n  * Don't use redirect_cache if allow_redirects=False\\n  * When passed objects that throw exceptions from `tell()`, send them via chunked transfer encoding instead of failing.\\n  * Raise a ProxyError for proxy related connection issues.\\n\\n## 2.9.1 (2015-12-21)\\n\\nBugfixes\\n\\n  * Resolve regression introduced in 2.9.0 that made it impossible to send binary strings as bodies in Python 3.\\n  * Fixed errors when calculating cookie expiration dates in certain locales.\\n\\nMiscellaneous\\n\\n  * Updated bundled urllib3 to 1.13.1.\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\nrequests/src/requests/exceptions.py at main This example uses `/workspace`:\n    \n    `mkdir -p /workspace\n    npm install -g @openai/codex@alpha`\n\n### Network access\n\nAllow outbound connections to these hosts:\n\n  * `https://api.openai.com` for environment registration.\n  * `wss://codex-cloud-environments.chatgpt.com` for commands and results.\n\n### Authentication\n\nUse `OPENAI_API_KEY` for application requests. Grant it `api.agents.read` and `api.agents.write` for session operations, plus `api.responses.write` for model inference. Add `api.vaults.read` and `api.vaults.write` if your application manages vaults.\n\nCreate a separate environment key on the Agents tab in the platform dashboard. It must belong to the same organization, project, and user or service account that owns the session. Set every other permission to None.\n\nSet `OPENAI_EXECUTOR_API_KEY` to this environment key in your application or provisioning service. Pass its value into the sandbox as `CODEX_API_KEY`, which `codex exec-server` reads. Keep your application’s `OPENAI_API_KEY` outside the sandbox.\n\nAgent-generated code can read the environment key, but the key only permits connecting environments. It cannot authorize any other API action. Keep it out of source code, container images, and logs. Rotate or revoke it when needed.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nCodex app-server – Sign in with ChatGPT | OpenAI Developers (https://developers.openai.com/siwc/token-sharing-open-source/codex-app-server)\nciteturn1search1 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; # Codex app-server ... If your app uses Codex app-server, configure it to send inference requests to the Responses API using an OAuth access token authorized for the user’s ChatGPT plan.\n\n# Codex app-server\n\nCopy Page\n\nConfigure Codex app-server to use an OAuth access token authorized for ChatGPT plan usage.\n\n## Using Codex app-server\n\nIf your app uses Codex app-server, configure it to send inference requests to the Responses API using an OAuth access token authorized for the user’s ChatGPT plan. You can use `model/list` to populate a model selector, but with the provider configuration below it can return a bundled client catalog. Treat it as a catalog, not an entitlement check; a successfully completed inference turn verifies access to the selected model for that request.\n\n  1. Pass the OAuth access token to the child process. After exchanging the authorization code, read the token response’s `access_token` field. Set `ACCESS_TOKEN` in the app-server child process’s environment to that value.\n\n  2. Start app-server with a Responses provider.\n    \n    `codex app-server --listen stdio:// \\\n      -c 'model_provider=\"openai_chatgpt_plan\"' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.name=\"ChatGPT plan\"' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.base_url=\"https://api.openai.com/v1\"' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.env_key=\"ACCESS_TOKEN\"' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.wire_api=\"responses\"' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.requires_openai_auth=false' \\\n      -c 'model_providers.openai_chatgpt_plan.supports_websockets=false'`\n\nCodex sends the user’s OAuth access token as `Authorization: Bearer <access_token>` on requests to `/v1/responses`. No separate Codex sign-in is required.\n\n  3. Drive the conversation over stdin/stdout. Send newline-delimited JSON messages. Start with `initialize` and include these fields in `params.clientInfo`:\n\n     * `name`: A stable identifier for your app, such as `my_app`. Codex uses this as the request originator for attribution. Use the same name across installations.\n     * `title`: Your app’s human-readable name, such as `My App`.\n     * `version`: Your app’s version, such as `1.2.3`. Codex includes it with the app identifier in the User-Agent.\n\nThese fields identify the calling app. The name should match the `agent_name_hint` that your app sends as part of new user registration flow. Wait for `initialize` to succeed, then send `initialized`. Send `thread/start` with your selected model and save `result.thread.id`. Send `turn/start` with that `threadId` and the user’s message. Display `item/agentMessage/delta` events. When `turn/completed` arrives, check `turn.status`: only `completed` indicates success; `failed` and `interrupted` do not.\n\n  4. Manage token renewal in your app. Obtain a replacement access token using the flow in Refreshing tokens. Restart app-server with the updated `ACCESS_TOKEN`, initialize the new process, and resume the conversation using `thread/resume` with the saved thread ID.\n\nYour app should read its own token file and supply the access token to the child process.--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nRun OpenAI agents on a Databricks Sandbox (https://developers.openai.com/cookbook/examples/agents_api/sandboxes/databricks/readme)\nciteturn1search2 [wordlim: 200] Published: 3 days ago; Crawled: 2 days ago; `codex exec-server` must stay running on the sandbox for the whole agent session. ...     async with AsyncOpenAI(api_key=app_key) as client: ...             environment={\"type\": \"self_hosted\", \"workspace_directory\": WORK_DIR}, ... The `codex exec-server` process is killed when a sandbox stops, and needs to be relaunched after sandbox restart to reconnect to the agent session.\n\nA sandbox can also be configured to auto-stop after it has been idle for `ComputeSpec.inactivity_timeout`. To disable sandbox auto-stop, set `inactivity_timeout` to 0 or leave the field unset.\n\n## Networking\n\nA sandbox has unrestricted outbound egress and closed inbound ingress. Both are currently unconfigurable.\n\nIf your OpenAI organization enforces an IP allowlist, add the sandbox’s outbound egress IP (see Databricks Outbound IPs). The egress IP is stable while a sandbox is running but can change across stop and restart cycles.\n\n## Monitoring\n\nThe executor writes a log inside the sandbox; read it with `execute_command_sync` (tail the exec-server log) to see registration and connection status or errors.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nPlugins | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/tools/plugins)\nciteturn1search3 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; ## Register plugins in a self-hosted sandbox ...         manifest = json.loads((plugin_directory / \".codex-plugin/plugin.json\").read_text()) ...                 root_dir=plugin_directory.parent,\n\n## Upload plugins to an OpenAI-hosted sandbox\n\nSupply one ZIP per plugin in `environment.plugins`. Image: Architecture diagram showing an application-owned interface, business context, and consent; Codex app-server agent loop and sandboxed execution; and application-owned MCP data and actions.\n\n## An open harness developers can inspect and adapt\n\nBecause the harness is open source, you can inspect the layer between your application and the model, understand how it behaves, and adapt the integration to fit your product.\n\nThat gives developers control over the parts that make the agent fit their product:\n\n  * The interface. A team can keep its existing dashboards, editors, queues, maps, records, and approval flows instead of forcing every interaction into a generic chat window.\n\n  * Context and tools. An application can expose the systems, documents, data, and actions that matter for a particular workflow, including application-owned MCP services.\n\n  * Operational boundaries. The host application can decide where an agent runs, which files or tools it can access, which actions require approval, how work is observed, and how results return to the system of record.\n\nWe publish the Codex CLI, app-server, and official Codex SDK as open-source components. Our open-source components guide lists what is available and where each component lives.\n\nThe open-source layer is the harness and integration surface; model access and managed services remain separate.\n\n## Choose the right integration layer\n\nBuilding on Codex does not require the same integration for every use case.\n\n  * For a script, CI job, or one-off background task, codex exec can run a bounded agent workflow and return structured output.\n\n  * For application code that needs to start, resume, or stream Codex tasks, the official Codex SDK provides a direct programmatic interface.\n\nFor a runnable example, see the Codex SDK documentation.\n\nUse Codex app-server when the agent is part of the product itself. It lets your application connect to a local Codex process, keep conversations open, stream events, interrupt work, expose tools, and respond to approval requests. The SDK simplifies common programmatic workflows; app-server gives product teams direct control over the lifecycle and user experience.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nArchitecture | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/architecture)\nciteturn1search5 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; An environment can be a remote sandbox, your laptop, a Docker container, or an AWS Lambda function.  * Application server: Your code that connects the agent to your product. ... Image: An application starts sessions and receives events from the Agents API, which runs the managed Codex harness and exchanges tool calls and results with a sandbox.\n\n# Architecture\n\nCopy Page\n\nUnderstand the harness, environment, and application server.\n\nOpenAI runs the agent harness. Your application sends it work and receives results. Add an environment when the agent needs compute or files.\n\n## The pieces\n\n  * Harness: The OpenAI-hosted Codex instance that runs the model and tool loop and maintains the agent’s session.\n  * Environment: Where the agent runs commands, executes code, and works with files. An environment can be a remote sandbox, your laptop, a Docker container, or an AWS Lambda function.\n  * Application server: Your code that connects the agent to your product. It submits tasks, receives events, and handles function tools. When you provide the environment, your code also manages its lifecycle.\n\nStart with the pieces your task needs. The harness can work without an environment, and your application can receive progress through streaming or webhooks.\n\n## Start without an environment\n\nAn agent that answers questions or uses tools to access external services may not need its own compute or files. OpenAI creates and manages a sandbox for the session.\n\nYou configure the packages, files, and network access the agent needs. The harness runs commands in the sandbox directly. Your application continues to send tasks, receive events, and handle any function tools.\n\nImage: An application starts sessions and receives events from the Agents API, which runs the managed Codex harness and exchanges tool calls and results with a sandbox. The application controls compute only for self-hosted sandboxes.\n\nThe dashed arrow applies only when you manage the environment yourself, as described below.\n\nSee OpenAI-hosted environments for configuration options.\n\n## Connect your own environment\n\nUse `environment.type: \"self_hosted\"` when the agent needs your infrastructure, private network, or custom software.\n\nYour code starts the environment and connects an executor to the session. The executor runs the commands and tools that the harness requests. Your application manages the connection and lifecycle without forwarding each command.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nE2B | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/environments/providers/e2b)\nciteturn1search6 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Create an isolated E2B sandbox with the session’s working directory and install the Codex CLI inside it.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nModal | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/agents-api/environments/providers/modal)\nciteturn1search7 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; Use the Modal SDK or API to create an isolated sandbox with the configured working directory.Install the Codex CLI in the sandbox, then start its executor with that environment ID and the environment key.\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nLocal shell | OpenAI API (https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/tools-local-shell)\nciteturn1search8 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: today; It’s designed to work with Codex CLI and `codex-mini-latest`. ... Both experiments pin commit **`7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a`** (2024-02-20). The historical snapshot makes Q5 reproducible; it is not the current release. Its four algorithm branches each define almost identical UTF-8 conversion and hexadecimal-digest functions. A future change to input conversion would require four edits. This is a concrete duplication problem in a real authentication path.\\n\\n`refactor.patch`, generated with `git diff -- src/requests/auth.py`, replaces the branch-specific functions with a five-entry constructor table and one local `hash_utf8` function. MD5-SESS deliberately shares the MD5 constructor; its session-specific calculation remains in the existing code. The function shrinks by 27 lines. No new algorithms or protocol behavior are introduced.\\n\\n## Tests before implementation\\n\\nI recorded 29 golden cases from the **unmodified** pinned source, then wrote `tests/test_digest_characterization.py`. Each case becomes a named `unittest` test. The committed `tests/digest_golden.json` contains literal observations, not values recalculated by the refactored code. Time and random bytes are fixed; tests compare the entire returned Authorization header and the nonce counter/last-nonce state.\\n\\nCoverage includes:\\n\\n- Default, MD5, MD5-SESS, SHA, SHA-256, SHA-512, including lowercase algorithm names.\\n- Missing/empty qop, `auth`, and multiple offered qop values; unsupported qop and whitespace behavior.\\n- Unicode credentials/realm, opaque values, an empty URL path, queries, fragments, and POST.\\n- Repeated and changed nonces, unknown/empty algorithms, and MD5-SESS without qop.\\n\\nThese are characterization tests: even the existing whitespace limitation is preserved. This is the first parent immediately before upstream's [fix merge `382fc2c0c6c0ef0874bc65bc1175f97c073e5086`](https://github.com/psf/requests/commit/382fc2c0c6c0ef0874bc65bc1175f97c073e5086), for [issue #6628](https://github.com/psf/requests/issues/6628). It is a real historical bug, not a deliberately injected fault. The issue describes failure to deserialize a JSON decoding exception and resulting process-pool failure. I read the issue, which also contains a proposed repair; this experiment does not claim a novel diagnosis.\\n\\nThe affected code is `src/requests/exceptions.py:JSONDecodeError`. Multiple inheritance selects `OSError`/`IOError`'s pickle reducer before the JSON decoder's reducer. The exception's display arguments omit the document and position needed by its constructor, so `pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(error))` raises `TypeError`. When a worker returns that exception, the parent cannot deserialize it and raises `BrokenProcessPool`.\\n\\n## My repair: capture, red, green\\n\\n“My repair” means the primary AI coding session's repair; no human-authored implementation is claimed. Before editing source, I wrote the six `unittest` methods in `tests/test_json_exception_pickle.py`:\\n\\n1. Three malformed response bodies (including Unicode/newlines) round-trip through all six supported pickle protocols.\\n2. A directly constructed exception round-trips.\\n3. Deep copy reconstructs the exception.\\n4. Existing message/document/position and catch hierarchy are characterized.\\n5. Valid JSON still decodes normally.\\n6. A spawned process propagates `JSONDecodeError`, then successfully runs another task in the same pool.\\n\\nThe tests compare exception type, backend compatibility, `msg`, `doc`, `pos`, `lineno`, `colno`, `args`, and string rendering. The first run had **20 subtest/assertion failures and one process-pool error across six methods**, while the two unaffected methods passed. This is preserved in `evidence/my-before.txt`.\\n\\nI then added `JSONDecodeError.__reduce__`, delegating to `CompatJSONDecodeError.__reduce__`. This preserves the appropriate constructor arguments with either standard-library `json` or optional `simplejson`. `my-fix.patch` is the actual source-only `git diff`. All six methods then passed, without changing the tests (`evidence/my-after.txt`). A separate environment reproduced the red/green cycle with simplejson 4.2.0.\\n\\nThe repair targets JSON decoding state. It does not promise preservation of arbitrary added attributes or attached request/response objects, nor does it change inheritance or valid JSON handling.\\n\\n## Independent agent comparison\\n\\nA **fresh `codex exec` session**, not a resume or fork, began from a clean checkout of the same faulty commit. I read the issue, which also contains a proposed repair; this experiment does not claim a novel diagnosis.\\n\\nThe affected code is `src/requests/exceptions.py:JSONDecodeError`. Multiple inheritance selects `OSError`/`IOError`'s pickle reducer before the JSON decoder's reducer. The exception's display arguments omit the document and position needed by its constructor, so `pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(error))` raises `TypeError`. When a worker returns that exception, the parent cannot deserialize it and raises `BrokenProcessPool`.\\n\\n## My repair: capture, red, green\\n\\n“My repair” means the primary AI coding session's repair; no human-authored implementation is claimed. Before editing source, I wrote the six `unittest` methods in `tests/test_json_exception_pickle.py`:\\n\\n1. Three malformed response bodies (including Unicode/newlines) round-trip through all six supported pickle protocols.\\n2. A directly constructed exception round-trips.\\n3. Deep copy reconstructs the exception.\\n4. Existing message/document/position and catch hierarchy are characterized.\\n5. Valid JSON still decodes normally.\\n6. A spawned process propagates `JSONDecodeError`, then successfully runs another task in the same pool.\\n\\nThe tests compare exception type, backend compatibility, `msg`, `doc`, `pos`, `lineno`, `colno`, `args`, and string rendering. The first run had **20 subtest/assertion failures and one process-pool error across six methods**, while the two unaffected methods passed. This is preserved in `evidence/my-before.txt`.\\n\\nI then added `JSONDecodeError.__reduce__`, delegating to `CompatJSONDecodeError.__reduce__`. This preserves the appropriate constructor arguments with either standard-library `json` or optional `simplejson`. `my-fix.patch` is the actual source-only `git diff`. All six methods then passed, without changing the tests (`evidence/my-after.txt`). A separate environment reproduced the red/green cycle with simplejson 4.2.0.\\n\\nThe repair targets JSON decoding state. It does not promise preservation of arbitrary added attributes or attached request/response objects, nor does it change inheritance or valid JSON handling.\\n\\n## Independent agent comparison\\n\\nA **fresh `codex exec` session**, not a resume or fork, began from a clean checkout of the same faulty commit. +# Q5 — Repairing Requests JSON exception serialization
+
+## System and real fault
+
+Project: [Requests](https://github.com/psf/requests), commit **`7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a`**. This is the first parent immediately before upstream's [fix merge `382fc2c0c6c0ef0874bc65bc1175f97c073e5086`](https://github.com/psf/requests/commit/382fc2c0c6c0ef0874bc65bc1175f97c073e5086), for [issue #6628](https://github.com/psf/requests/issues/6628). It is a real historical bug, not a deliberately injected fault. The issue describes failure to deserialize a JSON decoding exception and resulting process-pool failure. I read the issue, which also contains a proposed repair; this experiment does not claim a novel diagnosis.
+
+The affected code is `src/requests/exceptions.py:JSONDecodeError`. Multiple inheritance selects `OSError`/`IOError`'s pickle reducer before the JSON decoder's reducer. The exception's display arguments omit the document and position needed by its constructor, so `pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(error))` raises `TypeError`. When a worker returns that exception, the parent cannot deserialize it and raises `BrokenProcessPool`.
+
+## My repair: capture, red, green
+
+“My repair” means the primary AI coding session's repair; no human-authored implementation is claimed. Before editing source, I wrote the six `unittest` methods in `tests/test_json_exception_pickle.py`:
+
+1. Three malformed response bodies (including Unicode/newlines) round-trip through all six supported pickle protocols.
+2. A directly constructed exception round-trips.
+3. Deep copy reconstructs the exception.
+4. Existing message/document/position and catch hierarchy are characterized.
+5. Valid JSON still decodes normally.
+6. A spawned process propagates `JSONDecodeError`, then successfully runs another task in the same pool.
+
+The tests compare exception type, backend compatibility, `msg`, `doc`, `pos`, `lineno`, `colno`, `args`, and string rendering. The first run had **20 subtest/assertion failures and one process-pool error across six methods**, while the two unaffected methods passed. This is preserved in `evidence/my-before.txt`.
+
+I then added `JSONDecodeError.__reduce__`, delegating to `CompatJSONDecodeError.__reduce__`. This preserves the appropriate constructor arguments with either standard-library `json` or optional `simplejson`. `my-fix.patch` is the actual source-only `git diff`. All six methods then passed, without changing the tests (`evidence/my-after.txt`). A separate environment reproduced the red/green cycle with simplejson 4.2.0.
+
+The repair targets JSON decoding state. It does not promise preservation of arbitrary added attributes or attached request/response objects, nor does it change inheritance or valid JSON handling.
+
+## Independent agent comparison
+
+A **fresh `codex exec` session**, not a resume or fork, began from a clean checkout of the same faulty commit. It received only the symptoms and local execution constraints; it did not receive my tests, patch, solution, or the issue URL. Tool: **Codex CLI 0.164.0**; successful session model: **`gpt-6.1-sol`**, explicitly selected. See `evidence/agent-invocation.txt` and [ai-usage-log.md](ai-usage-log.md) for every prompt and complete responses, commands, outputs, and startup failures.
+
+**It did write and run a reproducing test before changing source.** In the successful JSONL transcript:
+
+| Event | Observed action |
+| --- | --- |
+| `item_4` | Added a parameterized regression test in upstream `tests/test_requests.py`. |
+| `item_5` | Ran it against faulty source: **12 failed**, with missing `doc` and `pos` constructor arguments. |
+| `item_6` | Added the source repair. |
+| `item_7` | New tests passed in a broader run, but one unrelated loopback-server fixture was blocked by the child's sandbox. |
+| `item_9` | A separate spawned-process check passed; the pool remained usable. |
+| `item_10` | Reran without server-dependent cases: **38 passed, 4 deselected**. |
+
+The agent tested both `Response.json()` decoding paths (`encoding=None` and `utf-8`) and all pickle protocols. It kept the regression test. Its source change is semantically identical to mine: both delegate to the backend JSON reducer; comments differ. My suite additionally checks multiple documents, direct construction, deepcopy, and persistent pool usability; its parameterized test covers the two decoding paths explicitly. `agent-fix.patch` includes **both** its source repair and its added test.
+
+Three startup attempts failed before any files were changed: a read-only Codex home, then socket-directory permissions in nested sandboxes. The fourth fresh session launched with automatic approval outside the outer sandbox and retained its own `workspace-write` sandbox. No bypass option was used. The complete failed-attempt logs are retained; they are not reported as successful repairs. The issue's historical report informed my repair, so this comparison measures the recorded workflows, not a controlled human-versus-AI study.
+
+## Independent verification and reproduction
+
+| Variant | Own suite, standard JSON | Own suite, simplejson | Relevant upstream JSON/pickle tests |
+| --- | --- | --- | --- |
+| Original | Expected red: 20 failures + 1 error | Expected red: 20 failures + 1 error | Agent's added test alone: 12 expected failures |
+| My patch | 6 passed | 6 passed | 12 passed, 330 deselected |
+| Agent patch | 6 passed | 6 passed | 24 passed, 330 deselected (includes 12 new cases) |
+
+`evidence/final-verification.txt` repeats this matrix in fresh cached clones, checks both submitted patches apply, and applies just the agent's test to original source to independently confirm it is red. The upstream test runs use a local httpbin plugin; no public HTTP endpoint is needed. An initial run lacked the plugin and failed at fixture setup; its actual output is retained in `evidence/upstream-missing-fixture.txt`, followed by successful runs after installing/loading the plugin. No full-upstream-suite claim is made.
+
+```bash
+python3 hw2/q5/reproduce.py \
+  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/requests-original \
+  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/venv/bin/python \
+  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/venv-simplejson/bin/python
+```
+
+Substitute your own cache and Python paths on another machine. Clone Requests outside the submission. Prepare Python 3.14 with Requests' dependencies, pytest 9.1.1, PySocks 1.7.1, and pytest-httpbin 2.1.0 (httpbin 0.10.4). For the second interpreter install simplejson 4.2.0 as well. Exact main packages are in `../q4/evidence/requirements-lock.txt`; the alternate environment is in `evidence/requirements-simplejson-lock.txt`. Omit the final argument to test only the standard JSON backend. All upstream execution and new clones stay beside the cached source; imports point explicitly to each tested clone.
+
+Review and security audit: [../q4/review.md](../q4/review.md).

+"""python3 reproduce.py CACHED_REQUESTS PYTHON [SIMPLEJSON_PYTHON].
+
+All clones and upstream test execution stay beside the cached upstream source.
+Python needs Requests dependencies, pytest, and pytest-httpbin (see report.md).
+"""
+import os
+from pathlib import Path
+import subprocess
+import sys
+import tempfile
+
+BASE = '7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a'
+HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
+
+
+def run(source, command, expected=0, marker=None):
+    env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONPATH=str(source / 'src'), PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD='1')
+    print(f'\nSource: {source} at {BASE}\nCommand: {command!r}', flush=True)
+    result = subprocess.run(command, cwd=source, env=env, text=True, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
+    print(result.stdout, end='', flush=True)
+    print(f'Exit code: {result.returncode}; expected: {expected}', flush=True)
+    if result.returncode != expected or (marker and marker not in result.stdout):
+        raise SystemExit('Unexpected verification result')
+
+
+def main():
+    upstream = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve()
+    pythons = [str(Path(p).absolute()) for p in sys.argv[2:]]
+    root = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='q5-verify-', dir=upstream.parent))
+    for variant in ('original', 'my', 'agent'):
+        source = root / variant
+        subprocess.run(['git', 'clone', '--quiet', '--no-hardlinks', str(upstream), str(source)], check=True)
+        subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(source), 'checkout', '--quiet', '--detach', BASE], check=True)
+        if variant != 'original':
+            patch = HERE / f'{variant}-fix.patch'
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', '--check', str(patch)], cwd=source, check=True)
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', str(patch)], cwd=source, check=True)
+        for python in pythons:
+            run(source, [python, '-c', 'import sys, requests; from requests.compat import JSONDecodeError; print(sys.version); print(requests.__file__); print(JSONDecodeError.__module__)'])
+            run(source, [python, '-m', 'unittest', 'discover', '-s', str(HERE / 'tests'), '-v'],
+                expected=1 if variant == 'original' else 0,
+                marker='missing 2 required positional arguments' if variant == 'original' else 'OK')
+        if variant == 'original':
+            # Apply only the agent's added test, leaving the faulty source intact.
+            patch = (HERE / 'agent-fix.patch').read_text()
+            test_patch = patch[patch.index('diff --git a/tests/'):]
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', '-'], cwd=source, input=test_patch, text=True, check=True)
+            run(source, [pythons[0], '-m', 'pytest', 'tests/test_requests.py', '-k', 'test_json_decode_error_is_pickleable', '-q', '-o', 'addopts='],
+                expected=1, marker='12 failed')
+        else:
+            run(source, [pythons[0], '-m', 'pytest', '-p', 'pytest_httpbin.plugin', 'tests/test_requests.py', 'tests/test_lowlevel.py', '-k', 'json or pickle', '-q', '-o', 'addopts='])
+            subprocess.run(['git', 'diff', '--check'], cwd=source, check=True)
+    print('Q5: original fails as expected; both independent repairs pass.', flush=True)
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    main()

+"""Regression tests for Requests issue #6628; no HTTP server is needed."""
+from concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor
+import copy
+import multiprocessing
+import pickle
+import unittest
+
+from requests import Response
+from requests.compat import JSONDecodeError as BackendJSONDecodeError
+from requests.exceptions import JSONDecodeError, RequestException
+
+
+def decode_invalid_json():
+    """A top-level function so the spawn multiprocessing context can import it."""
+    response = Response()
+    response.status_code = 200
+    response.encoding = 'utf-8'
+    response._content = b'{"answer":}'
+    return response.json()
+
+
+class JSONDecodeErrorPickleTests(unittest.TestCase):
+    def assert_same_json_error(self, original, restored):
+        self.assertIs(type(restored), JSONDecodeError)
+        self.assertIsInstance(restored, RequestException)
+        self.assertIsInstance(restored, BackendJSONDecodeError)
+        for field in ('msg', 'doc', 'pos', 'lineno', 'colno', 'args'):
+            self.assertEqual(getattr(restored, field), getattr(original, field), field)
+        self.assertEqual(str(restored), str(original))
+
+    def round_trip(self, error, protocol):
+        try:
+            return pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(error, protocol=protocol))
+        except TypeError as error:
+            self.fail(f'JSON exception could not be deserialized: {error}')
+
+    def test_response_errors_round_trip_all_pickle_protocols(self):
+        for body in (b'{"answer":}', b'{}{}', '{\n"資料": }'.encode()):
+            response = Response()
+            response.encoding = 'utf-8'
+            response._content = body
+            with self.assertRaises(JSONDecodeError) as caught:
+                response.json()
+            for protocol in range(pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL + 1):
+                with self.subTest(body=body, protocol=protocol):
+                    self.assert_same_json_error(caught.exception, self.round_trip(caught.exception, protocol))
+
+    def test_direct_constructor_round_trip(self):
+        original = JSONDecodeError('invalid value', '{\n"資料": }', 8)
+        self.assert_same_json_error(original, self.round_trip(original, pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL))
+
+    def test_deepcopy_uses_compatible_reconstruction(self):
+        original = JSONDecodeError('invalid value', '{\n"資料": }', 8)
+        try:
+            restored = copy.deepcopy(original)
+        except TypeError as error:
+            self.fail(f'JSON exception could not be copied: {error}')
+        self.assert_same_json_error(original, restored)
+
+    def test_current_error_metadata_and_catch_hierarchy(self):
+        original = JSONDecodeError('invalid value', '{\n"資料": }', 8)
+        self.assertIsInstance(original, RequestException)
+        self.assertIsInstance(original, BackendJSONDecodeError)
+        self.assertEqual((original.msg, original.doc, original.pos), ('invalid value', '{\n"資料": }', 8))
+        self.assertEqual((original.lineno, original.colno), (2, 7))
+
+    def test_valid_json_is_unchanged(self):
+        response = Response()
+        response._content = b'{"answer": 42}'
+        self.assertEqual(response.json(), {'answer': 42})
+
+    def test_worker_exception_does_not_break_process_pool(self):
+        with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, mp_context=multiprocessing.get_context('spawn')) as pool:
+            with self.assertRaises(JSONDecodeError) as caught:
+                pool.submit(decode_invalid_json).result(timeout=20)
+            self.assertEqual(caught.exception.doc, '{"answer":}')
+            self.assertEqual(caught.exception.pos, 10)
+            self.assertEqual(pool.submit(abs, -7).result(timeout=20), 7)
+
+
+if __name__ == '__main__':
+    unittest.main()

"""Run from anywhere: python3 reproduce.py CACHED_REQUESTS PYTHON_EXECUTABLE.

Creates a disposable clone beside the cached source; never modifies the input.
The supplied Python needs Requests' dependencies and pytest installed.
"""
import os
from pathlib import Path
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile

BASE = '7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a'
HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent


def main():
    upstream = Path(sys.argv[1]).resolve()
    python = str(Path(sys.argv[2]).absolute())
    scratch = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix='q4-verify-', dir=upstream.parent)) / 'requests'
    subprocess.run(['git', 'clone', '--quiet', '--no-hardlinks', str(upstream), str(scratch)], check=True)
    subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(scratch), 'checkout', '--quiet', '--detach', BASE], check=True)
    env = dict(os.environ, PYTHONPATH=str(scratch / 'src'), PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD='1')
    for phase in ('before', 'after'):
        print(f'\n{phase}: {scratch} at {BASE}', flush=True)
        for command in (
            [python, '-m', 'unittest', 'discover', '-s', str(HERE / 'tests'), '-v'],
            [python, '-m', 'pytest', 'tests/test_lowlevel.py', '-k', 'digest', '-q', '-o', 'addopts='],
        ):
            print(f'Command: {command!r}', flush=True)
            subprocess.run(command, cwd=scratch, env=env, check=True)
        if phase == 'before':
            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', '--check', str(HERE / 'refactor.patch')], cwd=scratch, check=True)
            subprocess.run(['git', 'apply', str(HERE / 'refactor.patch')], cwd=scratch, check=True)
    subprocess.run(['git', 'diff', '--check'], cwd=scratch, check=True)
    print('Q4: original and refactored behavior agree; upstream digest tests pass.', flush=True)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
"""Golden behavior captured before changing Requests' digest implementation.

No network access: pin time and randomness, then compare entire public headers
and the nonce state after each call. See digest_golden.json for all 29 cases.
"""
import json
from pathlib import Path
import unittest
from unittest.mock import patch

from requests.auth import HTTPDigestAuth


class DigestCharacterization(unittest.TestCase):
    pass


def make_test(case):
    def test(self):
        auth = HTTPDigestAuth(case.get('username', 'user'), case.get('password', 'pass'))
        auth.init_per_thread_state()
        challenge = {'realm': case.get('realm', 'realm'), 'nonce': 'nonce-A'}
        for key in ('algorithm', 'qop', 'opaque'):
            if case.get(key) is not None:
                challenge[key] = case[key]
        auth._thread_local.chal = challenge
        with patch('requests.auth.os.urandom', return_value=b'12345678'), patch(
            'requests.auth.time.ctime', return_value='Mon Jan 01 00:00:00 2024'
        ):
            for nonce, expected in zip(case.get('nonces', ['nonce-A']), case['observations'], strict=True):
                challenge['nonce'] = nonce
                with self.subTest(nonce=nonce):
                    actual = auth.build_digest_header(
                        case.get('method', 'GET'),
                        case.get('url', 'https://example.test/resource?x=1'),
                    )
                    self.assertEqual(actual, expected['header'])
                    self.assertEqual(auth._thread_local.nonce_count, expected['nonce_count'])
                    self.assertEqual(auth._thread_local.last_nonce, expected['last_nonce'])
    return test


for index, case in enumerate(json.loads(Path(__file__).with_name('digest_golden.json').read_text())):
    setattr(DigestCharacterization, f'test_{index:02d}_{case["name"].replace("-", "_")}', make_test(case))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    unittest.main()
# Q4 — Refactoring Requests digest authentication

Requests is an established, actively maintained Python HTTP library, with a substantial implementation and upstream test suite: [repository](https://github.com/psf/requests). Both experiments pin commit **`7a13c041dbef42f9f3feb14110f02626f6892e9a`** (2024-02-20). The historical snapshot makes Q5 reproducible; it is not the current release. Upstream source and environments live outside this submission under `/home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/`.

## Why this refactoring

`src/requests/auth.py:HTTPDigestAuth.build_digest_header` mixes algorithm selection, text encoding, digest calculation, challenge negotiation, and nonce state. Its four algorithm branches each define almost identical UTF-8 conversion and hexadecimal-digest functions. A future change to input conversion would require four edits. This is a concrete duplication problem in a real authentication path.

`refactor.patch`, generated with `git diff -- src/requests/auth.py`, replaces the branch-specific functions with a five-entry constructor table and one local `hash_utf8` function. MD5-SESS deliberately shares the MD5 constructor; its session-specific calculation remains in the existing code. The function shrinks by 27 lines. No new algorithms or protocol behavior are introduced.

## Tests before implementation

I recorded 29 golden cases from the **unmodified** pinned source, then wrote `tests/test_digest_characterization.py`. Each case becomes a named `unittest` test. The committed `tests/digest_golden.json` contains literal observations, not values recalculated by the refactored code. Time and random bytes are fixed; tests compare the entire returned Authorization header and the nonce counter/last-nonce state.

Coverage includes:

- Default, MD5, MD5-SESS, SHA, SHA-256, SHA-512, including lowercase algorithm names.
- Missing/empty qop, `auth`, and multiple offered qop values; unsupported qop and whitespace behavior.
- Unicode credentials/realm, opaque values, an empty URL path, queries, fragments, and POST.
- Repeated and changed nonces, unknown/empty algorithms, and MD5-SESS without qop.

These are characterization tests: even the existing whitespace limitation is preserved. They do not prove every possible input or authenticate against every server.

## Actual results

| Phase | Own characterization tests | Upstream `tests/test_lowlevel.py -k digest` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before source change | 29 passed | 3 passed, 17 deselected |
| After refactoring | 29 passed | 3 passed, 17 deselected |

The upstream tests exercise real loopback HTTP digest challenges, retry limits, redirects, and response-status handling. Logs with commands, commit and source paths are in `evidence/before-*.txt` and `evidence/after-*.txt`. `evidence/final-verification.txt` independently repeats both phases in a new clone and checks that the submitted patch applies. This is a targeted upstream run, not a claim that the entire Requests suite passed.

## Reproduce

Use Python 3.14.4. Requests' historical development requirements pin an old pytest; instead this experiment uses pytest 9.1.1 on Python 3.14. The exact installed packages are in `evidence/requirements-lock.txt`. The editable installation was built with setuptools 80.10.2 and wheel 0.48.0; install those before `pip install --no-build-isolation -e PATH_TO_REQUESTS`.

With the existing cached source and environment:

```bash
python3 hw2/q4/reproduce.py \
  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/requests-original \
  /home/yuankai/Projects/School/.cache/hw2-upstream/venv/bin/python
```

For another machine, clone `https://github.com/psf/requests.git` into a cache outside the submission, install `charset-normalizer`, `idna`, `urllib3`, `certifi`, `PySocks`, and `pytest` into a Python 3.14 environment, and substitute those two paths. The script pins the commit, creates a new sibling clone, runs tests before/after applying `refactor.patch`, and leaves the input checkout untouched. It sets `PYTHONPATH` to each tested clone to avoid accidentally testing another editable installation.

AI involvement and the review are disclosed in [ai-usage-log.md](ai-usage-log.md). Review findings and security checks are recorded in [review.md](review.md).

+        r._content = b'{"value":\n invalid}'
+        r.encoding = encoding
+        with pytest.raises(requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError) as excinfo:
+            r.json()
+
+        original = excinfo.value
+        restored = pickle.loads(pickle.dumps(original, protocol=protocol))
+
+        assert type(restored) is requests.exceptions.JSONDecodeError
+        assert isinstance(restored, RequestException)
+        assert isinstance(restored, JSONDecodeError)
+        assert restored.args == original.args
+        assert str(restored) == str(original)
+        for attr in ("msg", "doc", "pos", "lineno", "colno"):
+            assert getattr(restored, attr) == getattr(original, attr)
+
     def test_status_code_425(self):
         r1 = requests.codes.get("TOO_EARLY")
         r2 = requests.codes.get("too_early")
You will use test-driven development (TDD) to extend a simple calculator, Calc. Starter versions in Java, JavaScript, and Python are attached. Full instructions are in the PDF.

Required:

Question 1: add subtract, multiply, and divide one failing test at a time, and write a narrative of each red-green-refactor cycle.
Question 6: use an AI coding agent to add modulo and power, again with TDD. The rules:
The agent writes a failing test first.
You review and commit each test before the agent implements it.
Your tests must define the results for negative operands, modulo by zero, power(0, 0), and negative exponents before any code is written.
Report every time the agent broke a TDD rule, and every decision it made without asking you.

Optional:

Questions 2–5 cover CI, verification tools, refactoring, and fault repair. Questions 2 and 5 now also ask you to compare your work with an agent’s.
Question 7 checks whether code an agent writes from your tests only works for the test inputs.
Submit:

your tests;
your final Calc;
your git log;
a screenshot showing that all tests pass;
your narratives;
an AI usage log for Question 2-5 where you used AI, with every prompt verbatim and the agent’s full responses.
1. Calc currently implements one function: it adds two integers. Use test-driven design to add additional functionality to subtract two integers, multiply two integers, and divide two integers. First, create a failing test for one of the new functionalities, modify the class until the test passes, and perform any necessary refactoring. Repeat until all required functionality has been added to your latest version of Calc and all tests pass.
Remember that in TDD, the tests determine the requirements. Before modifying the software, you must encode decisions such as whether the division method returns an integer or a floating point number in automated tests. Submit printouts of all tests, your final version of Calc, and a screenshot showing that all tests pass. Most importantly, include a narrative describing each TDD test created, the changes needed to make it pass, and any necessary refactoring.
You can use any programming language to develop Calc. Please find attached Java, JS, and Python Calc implementations.

control for both source code and tests and populate both with a simple example.

code or adding a failing test case. Restore the build.
Then break the build again in the same way and ask an AI coding agent to make it
pass, without telling it what you changed. Report whether the agent fixed the

3. (Optional) Other than automated test execution. Extend the above exercise so that

4. (Optional) Find a refactoring in some large, existing system. Build tests that capture

tests still pass.

change and capture the current behavior with tests. At least one of these tests must
fail, thus demonstrating that you found the fault. Repair the fault and check that all
of your tests now pass.
Then ask an AI coding agent to repair the same fault, starting from the original
faulty code. Report whether it wrote a test that reproduces the fault before it

rules:
(a) In every cycle, the agent first writes one failing test. Show the failing test output
before any implementation of that behavior exists.
(b) You review each test before the agent implements it. Commit the reviewed
test, then let the agent make it pass in a separate commit. After a test is
committed, the agent may not change it unless you approve the change and
record why. Your git history must show this order.
(c) Before implementing, encode these decisions in automated tests: the result of
